The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`.\n- `order([\"a\"], [[\"a\",\"a\"]])` throws `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n- With `nodes [\"a\",\"b\",\"c\",\"d\"]` and edges `[[\"d\",\"b\"],[\"c\",\"d\"]]`, the result is `[\"a\",\"c\",\"d\",\"b\"]`.\n"}],"timestamp":1794124216714}}

The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`.\n- `order([\"a\"], [[\"a\",\"a\"]])` throws `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n- With `nodes [\"a\",\"b\",\"c\",\"d\"]` and edges `[[\"d\",\"b\"],[\"c\",\"d\"]]`, the result is `[\"a\",\"c\",\"d\",\"b\"]`.\n"}],"timestamp":1794124216714}}

Unknown node check — scan all edges before any ordering work,\n  // each edge as [before, after], before first then after.\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    if (!nodeSet.has(before)) {\n      throw new Error(`unknown node: ${before}`);\n    }\n    if (!nodeSet.has(after)) {\n      throw new Error(`unknown node: ${after}`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Build outgoing adjacency and indegree counts. Duplicate edges collapse\n  // naturally because adding the same destination twice to a set is idempotent\n  // and indegree is only increased when a new edge is recorded.\n  const out = new Map();\n  const indegree = new Map();\n  for (const node of nodeSet) {\n    out.set(node, new Set());\n    indegree.set(node, 0);\n  }\n\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    const dest = out.get(before);\n    if (!dest.has(after)) {\n      dest.add(after);\n      indegree.set(after, indegree.get(after) + 1);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Cycle check via repeated removal of nodes with indegree 0.\n  // Uses the same alphabetical selection, so path follows real edges.\n  const remaining = new Set(nodes);\n  const countRemaining = new Map(indegree);\n  while (remaining.size > 0) {\n    let next = null;\n    for (const node of remaining) {\n      if (countRemaining.get(node) === 0 && (next === null || node < next)) {\n        next = node;\n      }\n    }\n    if (next === null) {\n      // A cycle exists. Trace a cycle path following real edges.\n      const path = [];\n      const start = remaining.values().next().value;\n      let current = start;\n      const inPath = new Set();\n      while (!inPath.has(current)) {\n        inPath.add(current);\n        path.push(current);\n        // pick the alphabetically smallest outgoing neighbor still remaining\n        const neighbors = [...out.get(current)].filter((n) => remaining.has(n));\n        neighbors.sort();\n        current = neighbors[0];\n      }\n      // current is the first repeated node; rotate so path starts there.\n      const startIdx = path.indexOf(current);\n      const cycle = path.slice(startIdx);\n      cycle.push(current);\n      throw new Error(`cycle detected: ${cycle.join(\" -> \")}`);\n    }\n    remaining.delete(next);\n    for (const neighbor of out.get(next)) {\n      countRemaining.set(neighbor, countRemaining.get(neighbor) - 1);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Deterministic topological sort by alphabetically smallest available node.\n  const result = [];\n  const done = new Set(nodes);\n  done.clear();\n  const deg = new Map(indegree);\n  while (result.length < nodes.length) {\n    let next = null;\n    for (const node of nodeSet) {\n      if (!done.has(node) && deg.get(node) === 0 && (next === null || node < next)) {\n        next = node;\n      }\n    }\n    done.add(next);\n    result.push(next);\n    for (const neighbor of out.get(next)) {\n      deg.set(neighbor, deg.get(neighbor) - 1);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return result;\n}"},"partialJson":"{\"path\": \"/lclhome/iemma002/workspace/bench/stage/t8-s0-pi-deepseek-v4-flash-sandbox/src/topo.js\", \"content\": \"// Implement order(nodes, edges) here.\\nexport function order(nodes, edges) {\\n  const nodeSet = new Set(nodes);\\n\\n  // 1. Trace a cycle path following real edges.\n      const path = [];\n      const start = remaining.values().next().value;\n      let current = start;\n      const inPath = new Set();\n      while (!inPath.has(current)) {\n        inPath.add(current);\n        path.push(current);\n        // pick the alphabetically smallest outgoing neighbor still remaining\n        const neighbors = [...out.get(current)].filter((n) => remaining.has(n));\n        neighbors.sort();\n        current = neighbors[0];\n      }\n      // current is the first repeated node; rotate so path starts there.\n      const startIdx = path.indexOf(current);\n      const cycle = path.slice(startIdx);\n      cycle.push(current);\n      throw new Error(`cycle detected: ${cycle.join(\" -> \")}`);\n    }\n    remaining.delete(next);\n    for (const neighbor of out.get(next)) {\n      countRemaining.set(neighbor, countRemaining.get(neighbor) - 1);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Deterministic topological sort by alphabetically smallest available node.\n  const result = [];\n  const done = new Set(nodes);\n  done.clear();\n  const deg = new Map(indegree);\n  while (result.length < nodes.length) {\n    let next = null;\n    for (const node of nodeSet) {\n      if (!done.has(node) && deg.get(node) === 0 && (next === null || node < next)) {\n        next = node;\n      }\n    }\n    done.add(next);\n    result.push(next);\n    for (const neighbor of out.get(next)) {\n      deg.set(neighbor, deg.get(neighbor) - 1);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return result;\n}"}}],"api":"anthropic-messages","provider":"bench","model":"deepseek-v4-flash","usage":{"input":78,"output":929,"cacheRead":3072,"cacheWrite":0,"totalTokens":4079,"cost":{"input":0,"output":0,"cacheRead":0,"cacheWrite":0,"total":0}},"stopReason":"toolUse","timestamp":1794124219358,"responseId":"msg_b69f841430fe4e4ba68e0c4a"}}

{"type":"agent_end","messages":[{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Dependency Topological Sort\n\nCreate `src/topo.js` exporting one named ESM function, `order(nodes, edges)`. `nodes` is an array of string ids; `edges` is an array of `[before, after]` pairs meaning `before` must appear before `after` in the result. The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge.
